# Foundational Repositories — Education

**Pass p728 · verified 2026-11-09.** Building blocks rather than deployable platforms:
assessment engines, learner-model implementations, exam corpora and evaluation benchmarks.
Deployable systems (LMS, ERP, conferencing) are on `verticals/solutions.md`.

Stars, SPDX licence and `pushed_at` read this pass via `gh api -X GET search/repositories`;
existence confirmed on the raw channel. Where the search index has no entry for a
repository that demonstrably exists, that is stated in the row rather than treated as absence.

> 🔴 **`p727` added no row to this page, and that is a measured nil rather than a skipped leg.**
> The mandated foundational leg (`open source platform {industry} ERP CRM MIT Apache`) returned
> `openeducat/openeducat_erp`, `frappe/lms`, `OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT`, `francoisjacquet/rosariosis`
> and `OS4ED/openSIS-Classic` — **`5 / 5` already held on this page or on
> `verticals/solutions.md`.** The `grading agent LLM rubric` phrase leg returned **zero rows at
> all**. `p727`'s three finds were all *agents* and are filed on `agents/top.md`.
> 🟢 **What this page gained instead is four re-verifications, three of which contradict the
> search index and none of which contradict this base:** `openeducat_erp` indexes
> `NOASSERTION` and is **LGPL-3.0** by payload (`LICENSE`, `8,241 B`, read `2026-11-09` — the
> vendor's own LGPL claim is correct); `rasbt/LLMs-from-scratch` indexes `NOASSERTION` and is
> **Apache-2.0** (`11,505 B`, matching the `p687` cell); `OpenOLAT` indexes `NOASSERTION` and is
> **Apache-2.0** (`10,982 B`, matching `p676`); `OS4ED/openSIS-Classic` returns **`404` on all
> four root licence paths**, reproducing the standing finding that its terms exist only at
> `docs/License.txt` (`GPL-2.0`) where no root-filename probe can reach them.
> 🔵 **Three of four index fields are wrong; `4 / 4` of this base's payload-derived cells are
> right.** Recorded as the clearest evidence yet that reading the bytes is worth its cost.

## Learner modelling

| Repo | Stars | Licence | Last push | Why it is foundational |
|---|--:|---|---|---|
| [CAHLR/OATutor](https://github.com/CAHLR/OATutor) | 279 | MIT | 2026-11-08 | The open Bayesian Knowledge Tracing implementation. BKT is the mastery model most ITS work still benchmarks against; this is the auditable version of it. **North America.** |
| [GeminiLight/gen-mentor](https://github.com/GeminiLight/gen-mentor) | 136 | CC0-1.0 | 2026-10-23 | Skill-gap → learning-path generation as a multi-agent pipeline (WWW '25). CC0 means no attribution obligation at all. |
| [klieret/AnkiPandas](https://github.com/klieret/AnkiPandas) | 156 | MIT | 2026-11-03 | Reads and manipulates an Anki collection as **pandas DataFrames** — review logs, intervals, ease factors, lapses. 🔵 This base already held `ankimcp/anki-mcp-server` (`551★`, MIT), which *writes* cards; this is the half that **measures whether they worked**, which is the evidence layer `T226` says is now the buying criterion. Documented, long-maintained (since 2019). 🟢 **New at p724.** |
| [BennettSchwartz/membrane](https://github.com/BennettSchwartz/membrane) | 96 | MIT | 2026-11-05 | A **typed, revisable, decayable** memory substrate for agentic systems, with *competence* learning and trust-aware retrieval. `LICENSE`, `1,073 B`, © *2025 Bennett Schwartz*; normalised grant body `1,020 B` / sha256₁₂ `fe2a9817987f` — stock MIT by the `T255` test. 🔵 **Foundational for a reason the author does not advertise: decay plus a competence estimate is a learner model.** This base's two mastery implementations are `OATutor` (BKT, pedagogically principled, tied to its own React/Firebase app) and nothing reusable as a *service*. `membrane` is that missing piece — a general store whose primitives (per-item strength, decay over time, revision on new evidence, retrieval weighted by trust) are exactly the primitives a tutor needs per learner per skill, with no pedagogy baked in. 🟢 **New at p728.** ⚠️ **`96★`, created 2025, agent-infrastructure framing, no education citation**: it carries no mastery-model validity claim, so pair it with `OATutor`'s BKT for anything that must defend its mastery decisions (`P163`); use it alone only where the state is a convenience, not an assessment.|

## Evaluation benchmarks and exam corpora

A tutoring agent is only as trustworthy as the benchmark it is measured on. These are the
real, dated corpora.

|---|--:|---|---|---|
| [OpenLMLab/GAOKAO-Bench](https://github.com/OpenLMLab/GAOKAO-Bench) | 806 | Apache-2.0 | 2025-01-07 | Evaluates LLMs against China's Gaokao. Apache-2.0 and the de-facto APAC academic benchmark. Note: last push Jan 2025 — stable corpus, not active development. **APAC.** |
| [deekur/gaokaomath](https://github.com/deekur/gaokaomath) | 1,946 | CC-BY-4.0 | 2026-11-08 | Gaokao mathematics papers, 1952–2026. A 74-year longitudinal corpus; actively maintained. CC-BY-4.0 — attribution required, commercial use permitted. **APAC.** |
| [yunger7/enem-api](https://github.com/yunger7/enem-api) | 366 | GPL-2.0 | 2025-12-14 | Public open-source API over Brazil's ENEM exams and questions. The single best structured entry point to LATAM assessment content. **LATAM.** |
| [piresramon/gpt-4-enem](https://github.com/piresramon/gpt-4-enem) | 54 | MIT | 2024-12-06 | Code and data for evaluating LLMs on ENEM. Small and dormant, but MIT and the only published LATAM LLM-vs-national-exam harness found. **LATAM.** |
| [jangviktor-web/GongkaoNaoku](https://github.com/jangviktor-web/GongkaoNaoku) | 100 | **Apache-2.0 code / corpus NOT licensed** | 2026-10-25 | 考公脑库 — a spaced-repetition engine over **27,506 Chinese civil-service 行测 questions**. Zero third-party dependencies, single-file SQLite, native-JS SPA. 🔴 **Split verdict (`724-L`): the `LICENSE` payload was read and is genuine Apache-2.0 — the index is correct — but the README badge reads `许可-仅供个人学习` over the question bank.** Stage the engine; never the corpus. 🟢 **New at p724.** |
| [devissaputra/knowledge_tracing_benchmark](https://github.com/devissaputra/knowledge_tracing_benchmark) | **0** | MIT | 2026-11-08 | 🟢 **New at p723.** Benchmarks priors, **BKT, PFA and GRU** knowledge tracing on learner interaction data, in one MIT repository. ⚠️ **Zero stars — stated, not hidden.** It is filed because of what it *is*, not what it has: this base has carried `OATutor` as its only auditable BKT implementation, and comparing BKT against PFA and a neural baseline is precisely the evidence a procurement conversation now asks for (`T226`). Treat as a reference to read, not a dependency to adopt. |

## 🔴 The AI-output evaluation layer: the repository that fits the gap cannot be staged

This base has carried a declared gap for several passes — **no open-source evaluator for AI
output quality in an education context.** A repository that fits it exactly was found and
verified this pass, and it still cannot be staged:

| Repo | Stars | Index licence | Payload | Verdict |
|---|--:|---|---|---|
| [learning-commons-org/evaluators](https://github.com/learning-commons-org/evaluators) | 88 | `NOASSERTION` | **`LICENSE` → `404`; `LICENSE.md` → `404`** | 🔴 **Do not stage.** Evaluates AI output against *trusted educational rubrics* — functionally the missing piece, actively pushed (2026-11-07). But there is **no licence file at HEAD on either path**, so there is no grant to rely on. Usable as a design reference and as evidence the category is forming; not forkable, not client-deliverable. |

🔵 Per `722-F` the doc-path `404` does **not** mean the repository is absent — the index
confirms it alive at 88★. A missing `LICENSE` is the opposite problem from a missing README:
an undocumented library is still usable, whereas **an unlicensed one is not**, however
permissive its listing looks. `NOASSERTION` plus a `404` on every licence path is the
strongest available signal of *no grant at all*, and it is the one case where the correct
engineering answer is to reimplement the idea rather than adopt the code.

The gap therefore **stays open**, but it is now a *licensing* gap rather than a *supply* gap,
which is a different and more tractable problem: the rubric-evaluation pattern is public and
reimplementable under whatever licence Globant needs.

## Curriculum and pedagogy standards 🆕 new section at p725

The layer `T203` named as the chokepoint between a demo and a delivery, and the one this page
had no row for until this pass. Both rows are EMEA-authored, both are tiny, and neither is a
platform — they are **vocabulary and access**.

| Repo | Stars | Licence | Last push | Why it is foundational |
|---|--:|---|---|---|
| [isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP](https://github.com/isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP) | 13 | MIT | 2026-04-22 | MCP server over **all three Skolverket (Swedish National Agency for Education) open APIs**: Läroplan (curriculum), Skolenhetsregistret (school-unit register) and Planned Educations. 🔵 The only artefact in this base that reaches a **ministry's own curriculum data** as tool calls rather than as a scraped PDF — which is what turns "aligned to the national curriculum" from a claim into a query. TypeScript, stdio transport, published in the MCP registry. **Sweden — EMEA.** Also on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md). ⚠️ `13★`, single author, last push `2026-04-22`: pin a commit. |
| [YannHY/scenarisation](https://github.com/YannHY/scenarisation) | 7 | **CC-BY-SA-4.0** | 2026-11-01 | Learning-design application (after the UCL Learning Designer) that encodes the **pedagogical vocabulary** as data: eight competency frameworks — Socle commun, **DigComp 3.0**, **GreenComp**, CRCN, Pix, **Pix IA**, PER romand, Florimont — Bloom's taxonomy, 28 generic scenario templates in eight families, and **the AIAS AI Assessment Scale as a first-class field on every activity**. School-system referentials for France, Switzerland, the United States, the three Belgian communities, England, Wales, Scotland, Northern Ireland, the European Schools, the IB and **ISCED 2011**. PHP. **France/Switzerland — EMEA.** ⚠️ **CC-BY-SA-4.0 is a content licence, not a software grant**: take the framework and AIAS vocabularies as *data* (attribution + share-alike on derivatives of that data) and do not vendor the PHP into a client codebase on the assumption it behaves like MIT. |

## Interoperability clients — xAPI 🆕 new section at p726

`p613` filed the `Apache-2.0` xAPI / cmi5 **conformance** tier (US DoD / ADL). What it did not file
is the **client** tier: the libraries an application actually sends statements with. Both rows below
are published by **Rustici Software**, the vendor that authored SCORM and co-authored xAPI, so the
licence posture on the reference implementations is a planning fact rather than a preference.
⚠️ **Both are stale and both are fine for it** — a conformance client tracks a frozen specification,
so "no push in two years" means the spec did not move, not that the code rotted. Check the spec
version before the commit date.

| Repo | Stars | Licence | Last push | Why it is foundational |
|---|--:|---|---|---|
| [RusticiSoftware/TinCanJava](https://github.com/RusticiSoftware/TinCanJava) | 47 | Apache-2.0 | 2021-10-21 ⚠️ | xAPI (Tin Can) client for the JVM, from the vendor that wrote the specification. Payload read this pass (`LICENSE`, `11,358 B`). The JVM is where LMS integration work lands in public-sector EMEA and APAC estates. **North America (publisher).** |
| [RusticiSoftware/TinCan.NET](https://github.com/RusticiSoftware/TinCan.NET) | 49 | Apache-2.0 | 2023-08-30 ⚠️ | The same client for C#/.NET (`LICENSE`, `11,323 B`, read this pass). Filed because **.NET is the stack this base had no learning-record client for at all**, and a ministry or exam board running .NET cannot adopt the Java one. **North America (publisher).** |

Region **EMEA** (Germany; `b310 digital gmbh` in the payload and the README). 🔵 **Foundational because concept mapping is the one assessment format that reads a learner's *structure* rather than their answer** — the artefact an AI can mark for missing or wrong links, which multiple-choice cannot see. Self-hosted and permissive, so the maps stay on the institution's own infrastructure. 🟢 **New at p728.** ⚠️ No AI in it: this is the substrate, and the marking layer is the build.|

## Gaps — searched and genuinely missing

Recorded explicitly so a later pass does not read silence as coverage:

- **No permissively licensed open-source Student Information System.** Searched; every
  serious SIS is either proprietary or GPL/AGPL. For a client needing SIS customisation the
  honest answer is Odoo/OpenEduCat (LGPL) or build, not adopt.
- 🟢 **CLOSED at p725 — "no EMEA-authored foundational *building block*".** The gap is
  closed by two rows found this pass and filed above:
  [`isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP`](https://github.com/isakskogstad/Skolverket-MCP) (`13★`,
  **MIT**, Sweden) and [`YannHY/scenarisation`](https://github.com/YannHY/scenarisation)
  (`7★`, CC-BY-SA-4.0, France/Switzerland). 🔵 **Both are small, and both were invisible to
  every leg this bullet was ever based on**: the national-exam vocabulary (`Abitur`,
  `baccalauréat`, `Lernplattform`) does not reach them, and neither does any multi-term
  non-English phrase — see `725-D`. They were found by **single-term** legs (`skola`,
  `enseignant`). The honest summary of EMEA supply is now: **thin, small-n, and shaped as
  standards-and-vocabulary rather than as runnable platforms** — which is a different and more
  useful statement than a nil. Historical note: the narrow nil was re-asserted for five
  consecutive passes and the prior wider claim was withdrawn at p723. 🔴 **But the wider claim this bullet used to make — "EMEA supply in
  this industry is effectively absent from GitHub" — is withdrawn at p723 as false.**
  `Selleo/mentingo` (109★, **MIT**, pushed 2026-11-09) is a platform-scale, AI-native LMS from
  a Polish software house, and this base filed it at **p655** in the roster on
  [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) before re-asserting the nil four
  more times. EMEA supply is **thin and not of the building-block shape this page collects**
  — which is a real and useful finding — but it is not absent, and the five-pass nil was an
  artefact of the search vocabulary and of not re-reading this base's own rosters.
- **No open-source automated essay scoring that is both modern and maintained.** The
  category exists but is archaeological: the top results (`nusnlp/nea` 207★ GPL-3.0,
  `siyuanzhao/automated-essay-grading` 121★ MIT) were last pushed in **2018 and 2019**.
  Anyone needing AES in 2026 is building it on a general LLM, not adopting one of these.

The curated set above is the **verified-this-pass** shelf. The table below is the
**accumulated roster** from prior passes: 365 rows, every one carrying a real `github.com`
URL (checked: zero rows without one, zero placeholder rows). Preserved verbatim from p721.

Star counts, licences and default branches in it were read at the pass that filed each row,
**not today**. Re-verify a row before staking an engagement on it.

| Repo | Licence | Stars | Default branch | Why it is a foundation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [yunger7/enem-api](https://github.com/yunger7/enem-api) | 🟡 **GPL-2.0** (`LICENSE`, **`18,091 B`**, sha256₁₂ `f9c375a1be4a` — the full GPLv2 text, read from bytes at the default ref) | **366** | `main` | 🆕 **New at p721, and it is the LATAM supply this base keeps looking for in the wrong place.** A public, open-source API over the **ENEM** — Brazil's national secondary-education exam, the single highest-stakes assessment in the region — serving the real past papers and their questions. 🟢 **This is the artefact that makes a Brazilian tutoring pilot evaluable:** every LATAM tutor row on the sibling shelf can be scored against actual ENEM items instead of synthetic ones, which is the difference between a demo and a measurable pilot. 🔴 **Licence gate: GPL-2.0 is copyleft — consume it as a NETWORK SERVICE, never vendor the code into a client deliverable.** Linking its code into a proprietary studio build propagates the grant; calling it over HTTP does not. 🟡 Last push `2025-12-14`, so the question corpus is current to the 2025 cycle and must be re-checked before a 2026-cycle pilot. Region placed on the national exam it serves, not on author geography. |
| [`tough-dev-school/education-backend`](https://github.com/tough-dev-school/education-backend) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,097 B`, sha256₁₂ `653eee04ff8b`, raw bytes at `master`) | **`489`** | `master` | 🆕 **New at p720 — and it is the ONLY LIVE SURVIVOR of a field this pass measured and found to be a graveyard; see `720-D`.** A Django **production** LMS backend, not a demo: Python 3.14, deps via `uv`, CI plus a separate crossplatform CI workflow, and real integrations (Tinkoff payments, Dashamail, Postmark, S3, Telegram). Frontend lives separately at [`tough-dev-school/lms-frontend-v2`](https://github.com/tough-dev-school/lms-frontend-v2). 🟢 **Why it is a foundation rather than an agent: it supplies the enrolment, payment, cohort and delivery substrate that every tutor agent on the sibling shelf assumes and none provides.** Region `EMEA` read from the artifact — Tinkoff and Dashamail are Russian-market services, which is a provenance signal and also a procurement constraint worth naming before it is proposed to a client. |
| [`HKUDS/DeepTutor`](https://github.com/HKUDS/DeepTutor) | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE`, `11,408 B`, sha256₁₂ `cd2f54e1e506`, raw bytes at `main`) — 🔵 **`50 B` over canonical and CHECKED: the `APPENDIX` is filled in, and `APPENDIX`, `Disclaimer of Warranty` and `Limitation of Liability` are all present.** Complete Apache. | **`57,248`** | `main` | 🆕 **New at p720 at the top of this shelf by two orders of magnitude, and it arrives WITH A CORRECTION.** A November-2026 GitHub-trending roundup reports it at *"about 45K stars"*; read from the API this pass it is **`57,248`** — a `27%` understatement, and this KB had been carrying the prose figure. 🟢 **The grant's own `APPENDIX` settles the region the prose never did: *"Copyright 2025 Data Intelligence Lab, The University of Hong Kong"* → `APAC`.** Personalised AI tutoring positioned as lifelong learning; active (`pushed 2026-11-07`). 🔵 **Read the licence file for provenance, not only for permission — it is frequently the most reliable affiliation statement in a repository.** |

Filed as a foundation because the rules-engine-plus-narrator skeleton transfers to any procedural subject; the card game is incidental. |

| [`JacobFV/formal-language-cirriculum`](https://github.com/JacobFV/formal-language-cirriculum) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,069 B`, sha256₁₂ `7ea5cac8cd6a`, read from the bytes at `master`) | `0` | `master` | 🆕 **New at p719** · region **Global** (🔴 REFUSED: `Spanish`, `Swahili` and `Chinese` appear only as *curriculum targets*, never as origin or deployment). A formal-language curriculum where **every lesson is a GENERATOR, not a dataset** — an episode is a pure function of a seed, the vocabulary is invented per episode, the grammar/ontology/proof-calculus is sampled fresh, and the answer is **computed from the construction rather than annotated by anyone**. 🟢 **Why this is a foundation and not a course: it produces an evaluation set that CANNOT have been trained on, because it did not exist until it was asked for.** That is the single hardest problem in assessing an education agent, and this is the only subject on these shelves that solves it structurally instead of by holding out data. Profile runs from reference, equivalence and discrimination up through recursion, parsing, variable binding and quantification to proof, argument and value inference. 🔵 **Count instability recorded rather than resolved:** the `master` README headlines `180 lessons` (×3) but also says `179 lessons` once in the same file, and the repository description says `170`. Three figures, one subject — the generator design is what the row is for; do not cite a lesson count from this shelf. |
| [`Grvrajput/llm-eval-rubric-kit`](https://github.com/Grvrajput/llm-eval-rubric-kit) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,069 B`, sha256₁₂ `313f8cce1ca8`, read from the bytes at `main`) | `0` | `main` | 🆕 **New at p719** · region **Global** (🔴 REFUSED: no institutional or locative token in the `main` README). A dependency-free toolkit that turns **binary PASS/FAIL criteria** into a weighted score, a 1–5 rating and evidence-bearing rationales. 🟢 **The design decision that makes it a foundation is that it is JUDGE-AGNOSTIC: the caller decides how each criterion is judged — human, heuristic or LLM-as-judge — and the library does only the weighting, scoring and rating MATH, deterministically.** That separation is exactly what an education engagement needs and almost never gets: the grading *policy* stays auditable and reproducible while the *judgment* stays swappable. Encodes three rules in the data model — criteria binary and checkable, weighting explicit, every judgment carries a rationale. 🟢 **Composes directly with the generation rows on [`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md)**: it is the scoring half that `GyanKosh`'s and `StudyForge`'s teacher-review workflows leave unspecified. Ships CI. |

A **backend-only** exam-generation engine that takes scope text, course notes and an exam format and emits a structured exam **blueprint** plus a question stream. 🟢 **Its stated value proposition is the reason it is a foundation: not generating questions but MANAGING THE RELIABILITY OF GENERATION.** It reduces loose LLM output to strict domain rules, keeps the flow alive on malformed or incomplete output with fallback plus retry, and exposes quality as **SSE metadata** so the pipeline is observable rather than opaque. 🟢 **Self-positioned as an engine core and not a product** (`Public Beta`, `tam ürün değil`), with LMS and assessment-pipeline teams named as the intended consumers — which is precisely the integration posture a studio wants in a dependency. |

(2) It ships an **agentic Alexa+ simulator — an LLM agent that discovers the server's tools over real MCP and decides for itself which to call** — which is a genuine integration *test harness* for tool descriptions, not a scripted demo. 🔵 **Read the public instance carefully:** it runs in offline keyword mode with data reset on restart, and the Bedrock path is opt-in via `.env`. The deployable artefact is the server, not the hosted URL. |

Uses Git commit attribution, so provenance is derived rather than asserted. |

Take the rule table and the descriptor state machine; do not take the harness. |

Owner expansion on this convention is the cheapest supply lead p719 leaves behind, and it is left deliberately rather than spent here. |

No added clause.** | **685** | `master` | 🆕 **New at p706 — the largest subject in this pass's intake (`685★`, `103` forks) and the one this base scrutinised hardest, because its README makes the kind of claim this base normally refuses.** A TypeScript agent framework: persistent **cognitive memory with Ebbinghaus decay**, runtime **tool forging** (the agent writes a function, declared test cases run it, an LLM judge reviews, approved tools join the session), optional HEXACO personality, `7` orchestration strategies, `13` LLM providers. 🟢 **Why it survived scrutiny instead of being refused as over-claiming: the benchmarks are falsifiable and published.** `85.6%` on LongMemEval-S and `70.2%` on LongMemEval-M, with a **separate public bench repository** and a stated per-correct cost — a claim pinned to a reproducible method is a different object from an adjective. CI, codecov and npm releases all present. 🔵 **The education relevance is NOT generic, and it is the reason this is filed here:** Ebbinghaus decay is the same forgetting curve that the FSRS/Anki retention layer already on this shelf implements for *learners*. **This framework applies it to the AGENT's memory** — so a long-running tutor can forget and reconsolidate on the same principle its scheduler uses on the student. 🔴 **Not education-specific, and filed as substrate only.** |

It earns a foundation row because it is the permissive *substrate* under the one thing this shelf's education models keep needing and almost never get permissively: **post-training**. Concretely relevant features: **zero-intrusion agent access** (an agent only has to call a standard chat-completion interface — multi-turn context concatenation, tool-output recognition and loss masking are the framework's job, not the agent's), **multi-paradigm online distillation** (PG-style, GKD-style and mixed, with TopK / full-vocabulary / JSD KL strategies) and **multi-data-source mixed training** where agents, reward functions, teacher models and sampling hyper-parameters are configured *per data source* with controllable mixing ratios. 🟢 Read in one line: this is how a tutor built on a `4B` model like [`CogEvol/CogEvol-4B`](https://github.com/CogEvol/CogEvol-4B) gets *distilled from* a frontier teacher under a licence Globant can ship — and the per-data-source mixing is what lets one run blend a maths reward, a Socratic-dialogue reward and a curriculum-alignment reward instead of collapsing them into one scalar |
| [`Ta0p1/ResolveLab`](https://github.com/Ta0p1/ResolveLab) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,068 B`, sha256₁₂ `2bbd31572d7f`, read from the bytes at `main`; holder *“Copyright (c) 2026 Allen Huang”*) | **104** | `main` | 🆕 **New at p705, and like the row above it is not an education project — it is the assessment METHOD this base has been describing in prose for several passes, implemented.** ResolveLab runs tool-using agents against a **real HTTP service that injects faults**, and grades the episode **from server state, the commit log and read versions — never from the agent's claim**. The README's framing is the transferable sentence: an agent *“can produce valid JSON, sound confident and still leave the system wrong.”* Faults are chosen so they **change the right next action**: paginated lookup with near-miss references, `If-Match` conflicts (`412`), `Retry-After` (`429`), a `502` **after** a committed write, permission revoked mid-task (`403`), a concurrent operator finishing the job, already-correct tickets, and instructions injected into ticket text. **Side-effect grading** fails wrong-record writes, redundant commits, missing fresh reads, false success and unjustified escalation **even when the final record looks right**. Evidence discipline is unusually strong: studies are **registered before any model run**, test inputs and weights are **hash-locked before testing**, and every published episode **replays over real HTTP without a GPU** — its own `v0.5`/`v0.4` protocols and results are in-repo. 🟢 Why a foundation for *education*: swap tickets for a marking task and this is **authentic assessment** — grade what the learner (or the marking agent) actually did to the system, not what they asserted — and it is the only permissive harness on this shelf that treats *“the answer looked right”* as a failure mode to be measured |

| [fasiha/ebisu](https://github.com/fasiha/ebisu) | 🟢 **Unlicense / public domain** — `UNLICENSE`, `1,211 B`, sha256₁₂ `7e12e5df4bae`, *"free and unencumbered software released into the public domain"* | **338** | `gh-pages` | 🆕 **New at p686 — the scheduling primitive this KB had an opinion about but no alternative to.** This base cites **FSRS** `10×` on the agent shelf and `11×` here, and has never carried a second model of forgetting. Ebisu is that second model, and it differs in kind rather than in tuning: FSRS fits a trained parameter set to review logs, while Ebisu keeps a **Beta-distributed posterior over recall probability per item** and updates it in closed form, so it returns *calibrated uncertainty* with every prediction and needs **no training corpus at all**. That makes it the one retention primitive on this shelf that is deployable against a cold start — a new course, a new language pair, a client with no review history. Ports exist in JS, Java and Dart. ⚠️ **Two cautions, both measured:** last push `2024-10-02` (`25` months — the staleness is real, the mathematics is not time-sensitive), and its default ref is **`gh-pages`, not `main`** — a tree-reading pipeline that assumes `main`/`master` will silently read nothing here. 🟢 Public domain is *strictly more permissive* than MIT: no attribution clause to carry into a client deliverable. |

The `oficial-seca-2026-09` round published **19 models × 900 responses each = 17,100 raw responses, committed one by one**, with a **private held-out set against contamination**. Headline spread: the rate of responses faithful to the official text runs from **88% down to 3%** depending on the model. The score is the mean of five dimensions — recognising real codes, refusing fake ones, text fidelity, reverse lookup, and correct citation in open generation — and *refusing a fake code* is scored separately from *reproducing a real one*, which is the distinction a procurement test actually needs. `v0.3.0`, `82` commits, `12★ / 1 fork`. 🔴 **Stale self-description, recorded:** the repository's About text still says **17 models / 15,300 responses** while the README says **19 / 17,100** — the run grew and the About did not follow. Cite the README figure. 🔵 **LATAM-placed** on the strongest channel there is: the artefact *is* a national curriculum standard, and the grant payload names **`Profy Tecnologias Educacionais Ltda`**, a Brazilian legal-entity form. |

That is a *measurement* answer to **T10** (“the evidence base remains the sector's weak point”) and **T19**, and it is the first one on this page that an EMEA public buyer can run without egress. With `jupyterhub` (BSD-3) and `nbgrader` (BSD-3) already shelved, this completes a **BSD-all-the-way-down technical-curriculum stack that launches from inside the client's existing LMS.** Lowest star count on this shelf (73); it is 230 lines of glue doing disproportionate architectural work. |

Built and run by **TUM** (Technical University of Munich) for its own teaching — production university software, not a paper's companion code. **395 forks, 182 open issues, pushed 2026-11-05, latest tag `10.3.1`.** 🟢 **The AI layer is in-tree and configuration-gated**: **Iris**, an LLM virtual tutor that guides with hints and leading questions; **Athena**, which *suggests* feedback for text, modeling and programming exercises; **Hyperion**, AI-assisted exercise authoring built on Spring AI. Every deck is re-validated after generation against six metrics for completeness, on-topic-ness, ordering and teacher-editability, and the whole constraint set is a single `UnitConstraints` object driving both the prompt and the validator. Ships a contract compiler, a validator and a benchmark harness with a paper. |
| [RAFPAPER2026/RAF](https://github.com/RAFPAPER2026/RAF) | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** (`LICENSE`, `11,358 B`, sha256₁₂ `cfc7749b96f6`, canonical text) | **0** | `main` | 🆕 **p703 — automatic rubric generation with a human-alignment measurement, which is the assessment primitive this base has repeatedly named as a GAP.** Paper release in two parts: **RAF**, an LLM-driven platform that generates **AND–OR rubric trees** from a query, records human and judge scores, measures alignment with human preference, and refines the rubric iteratively through expert-reviewed suggestions (PROMPT-R) with failure-mode diagnostics (RIFT); and **ClawBench**, `121` long-horizon personal-assistant tasks with a Docker evaluation harness, a judge agent, a manual scoring UI and reporting tools. Rubrics are verified and embedded in the task configs: **`1,920` binary leaf criteria (`666` completion-side, `1,254` quality-side) over `533` internal AND/OR group nodes**, bilingual (Chinese task definitions with English translations). 🔴 **Two caveats, both stated rather than discovered later: the owner name `RAFPAPER2026` is a paper-anonymisation account with no identity behind it and no history, and the task domain is personal-assistant work, not education** — the AND–OR rubric tree and the human-alignment loop are what transfer, not the benchmark. |
| [michael-borck/assessment-bench](https://github.com/michael-borck/assessment-bench) | 🟢 **MIT** (`LICENSE`, `1,070 B`, sha256₁₂ `b4fdbc9f39ce`) | **0** | `main` | 🆕 **p703 — APAC, and it is the instrument this base should have had before trusting ANY LLM marking claim.** Runs one cohort through competing assessment arms — pure-LLM marking as the baseline, hybrid LLM-plus-deterministic-signals, and a deterministic signals-only arm — with **repeated runs**, and reports per-submission consistency (mean, median, standard deviation, coefficient of variation, reliability) plus Pearson and Spearman agreement of every arm and every numeric signal **against human marks**. 🔵 **Its self-description is the discipline: "the bench measures; it never marks."** It exists to answer *how consistent is LLM marking across repeated runs and providers* and *which deterministic signals actually track human judgement* — the two questions that decide whether an AI-marking engagement is defensible.
